    This is Survivor: Old Blood versus New Blood. Nine returning players, nine of the biggest fans of the show, but with one timely twist. The nine players coming back to Survivor not only have experience in the game, but experience in life, all over the age of 35. The new players have fresh eyes Survivor and also fresh eyes on this world, all under 35. How will the wisdom of age and experience play against players who are young, scrappy, and hungry? Find out when these players are all on one island, battling each other and the elements until only one person is left standing to claim the million dollar prize.

    39 Days.

    18 Americans.

    One Survivor.

    Day One Agatha spits the sand out of her mouth. She’s so close to the finish line, she can taste it. She reaches out an arm toward the line in the sand, but can’t quite make it, due to this pesky random lady from the other tribe, semi- effectively pinning her down.

    Minutes after they landed in Fiji, sadist extraordinaire Jeffrey Botox Probst made them participate in a challenge for flint, a one-on-one capture the flag of sorts against the New Blood tribe. Which currently has Agatha seconds away from a point if it wasn’t for said woman on top of her. With her arm that isn’t clutching her stupid tribe flag, she simply and purposely elbows the bitch off of her.

    “Oooh and a rough hit to Wanda from Agatha!” Jeff calls. Damn right it is. Without the weight of this Wanda on her, Agatha easily drags herself across the finish line. “And that’s a point for Agatha for Old Blood! Minutes into this game and we are already playing dirty.”

    Agatha rolls her eyes. She did miss some things about this game—mainly exploiting the stupidity of her fellow contestants and how Rio looks smiling underneath the stars—but she sure as shit didn’t miss Jeff’s commentary. “Playing dirty?” she counters. “Moi?” Then, to prove her point, she holds out a hand to help her opponent up. “I just got a little carried away. Happens to the best of us, right Willa?”

    “Wanda,” Wanda corrects. She eyes Agatha’s hand suspiciously for a second before taking it.

    “Tough break, toots,” Agatha says with the grin of a victor, as she pulls Wanda up. “You’ll get ‘em next time.”

    Wanda doesn’t say anything, eyes still on firmly on Agatha, like she’s trying to figure her out. Agatha pays her no mind. This bitch might not even make the merge, it’s doubtful she’s worth Agatha’s brain space. Agatha looks away from Wanda, raises her flag above her head in victory, and jogs back to her tribe. Back to Rio, waiting for her with a scowl. The most gorgeous scowl Agatha has ever seen, but still definitely a scowl. “Toots?” Rio mutters as Agatha goes to stand besides her. “What was that?” “Just being friendly,” Agatha whispers back. She raises an eyebrow. “Jealous?”

    “No,” Rio says in a way that means yes. Her mouth is a hard line. Oh, sweet Rio. Agatha wants to kiss the frown off of her face, wants to hold her close and tell her, dramatic and romantic, “darling, how could I even look at any other fool in this game when you are beside me?” But the cameras are rolling and the show is ongoing, so Agatha doesn’t do that. Instead, she leans over to whispers in Rio’s ear, too quiet for the cameras, “trust me, she’s not the one I’ll be taking into the woods tonight and making scream—”

    “There it is!” Jeff yells. “Just inside the line, Nick scores for New Blood. We are 1-1!”

    Agatha turns back to the challenge to see some kid with shaggy hair and a big t-shirt who barely looks 15, grinning and holding up a flag, while Todd from Old Blood slinks back to their side.

    “Up next,” Jeff says. “Rio from Old Blood vs. Kate from New Blood.” Agatha, with an iron will, resists the urge to smack Rio’s ass as she goes up to the arena.

    She’s gotten too comfortable at home, with Rio everywhere. Smiling that big smile that’s painted on her face now, as she’s about to kick some kid’s ass. It’s become second nature to fully exist in each other’s space with no barriers, a touch on a back, a mouth on a neck, Rio’s hands on Agatha’s thighs as she knelt at the foot of their bed all those months ago, after they came home from the Solomon Islands reunion.

    “I need to know that you mean it,” Rio said that night, looking up into Agatha’s eyes. She’s always been so good at that, at articulating her thoughts and wants in a straight line, while Agatha has grown quite accustomed to expressing her own desires through a labyrinth like she's on a quest to slay a Minotaur.

    “It can’t just be for the game,” Rio said plainly. “I love the game, I love the way you play it, but I love you more. Most. Best. I can’t… if we do this—if we sign papers that say forever, it needs to be because we mean forever.” How free it must have be for Rio, to express something so big, so monumental like that, with a simple earnestness that Agatha has never fully grasped.

    Which is maybe why Agatha felt like a child when she asked Rio, voice sounding too small and too weak, “do you? Do you really mean forever with me?

    Rio looked up at her then, those big brown eyes so full of affection that Agatha could hardly bear to look at them.

    “Agatha,” she said softly. Sometimes, Agatha couldn’t stand the way Rio said her name, like no one else had before, filled with a reverence and tenderness Agatha never thought she would be on the receiving end of. “Agatha, sweetheart, of course I do. God, I would have proposed to you on that goddamn island if you wouldn’t have laughed right in my face.” But Agatha wouldn’t have laughed in her face. She just wouldn't have believed her. Wouldn’t have believed that someone could see every part of her on display out there and still entertain the idea of forever. But there Rio was, knees on the hard floor of the house she built herself, in this life she made herself, asking Agatha to share it with her. And Agatha, once again, found herself unable to resist this woman. She reached her hand out, cupped Rio’s face and whispered, soft and honest in the way she only was with Rio, “of course it’s not just for the game, my love.”

    Rio’s face broke out into the biggest smile imaginable. The most beautiful person in the world on her knees in front of Agatha, loving her like it was second nature.

    “Making it look easy,” Jeff is yelling, “Rio scores for Old Blood. We are 2- 1!”

    And here Rio is, covered in sand and grinning like a madwoman as she heads back toward Agatha.

    How terrifyingly easy she is to love.

    Especially because Agatha knows how these things tend to go. How papers that mean forever can only do so much.

    But she can’t focus on that right now. She has a million dollars to win. Which is also, tragically, the reason she can’t take Rio in her arms, here on this beach and kiss the sand off her lips.

    Because they have a plan.

    This time, they aren’t fucking around. This time, they are winning Survivor.

    Day 10 “Drop your buffs,” Jeff calls, “we are mixing things up!” “Must we?” Agatha asks, rolling her eyes. Thank god she doesn’t have to pretend to be Sad Meek Wife this time around. This time she gets the genuine pleasure of pissing Jeff off just for fun.

    “We must!” Jeff says, grinning through his annoyance. Perfect. This “mixing things up” is barely a twist; themed seasons tend to have a tribe swap these days. Their plan has accounted for a tribe swap, and the pieces have already been set in motion. Agatha has already found and pocketed an idol, and Rio carved a fake one for Agatha to plant on Matt. Their game is going extremely well, and will continue to go extremely well, even if God forbid, Agatha has to play this game apart from Rio. But Agatha doesn't want to. She’s gotten used to playing Survivor side by side with Rio, having someone to talk with, to bounce ideas off of, to ravish in the jungle. It would be a real shame for that part of the game to be taken away from her.

    “We are doing this schoolyard pick style,” Jeff is saying. “Go ahead and draw rocks. One person will pick an orange rock and one will pick a blue rock. Those will be team captains.”

    When they reveal, an orange rock stares up at Agatha from her palm. Perfect.

    “Alright,” Jeff is saying. “Captains, step onto your mats. Agatha, you are the captain of the new Sakaar tribe. Kate, you are the captain of the Pyree tribe. You are both going to pick someone from the tribe you were not on. Agatha, you’ll pick someone from former New Blood, Kate will pick someone from former Old Blood, and so on. Agatha, you’re up.” Agatha eyes the New Blood tribe. If she had it her way, her first pick would be Rio and none of the others would matter. But tragically, she has to pick from this crop of children, all of whom look worse for wear after only ten days. Agatha’s eyes fall on the woman who looks like she belongs least in the New Blood tribe.

    “Wanda,” Agatha says definitively. In the brief moments they have been exposed to the other tribe, Agatha noticed Wanda tearing up at New Blood’s first and only immunity win, blubbering something about how she was here for one son or two sons or a husband who may or may not be alive. It was a tender display of vulnerability that Agatha can use down the line if she needs to.

    Clear shock paints Wanda’s face after Agatha calls her name, but she covers it up with a smile as she heads over to Agatha.

    “Thanks,” she says a bit warily. Then, clearly trying to be friendly, adds, “my son loves you.”

    “Gay?” Agatha asks. She’s aware of her fanbase.

    “Well, I mean—I don’t—he’s only 13,” Wanda says awkwardly. Oh, she for sure knows her son is gay.

    “They start young these days,” Agatha says with a grin.

    “Kate, you’re up!” Jeff calls. “You have to pick someone from Old Blood.” Agatha glares at this Kate, all young and eager and fit. If this little bitch chooses Rio…

    ”Oh wow, really tough choice,” Kate is saying, “such amazing players.” Kate’s eyes land on Agatha and Agatha narrows her eyes, bares her teeth. Kate swallows and looks away.

    “Matt!” she calls. Smart girl.

    “Wanda, you’re up!” Jeff calls. “Picking someone from Old Blood.” Agatha wonders what sort of death threats she can get away with on broadcast TV to make Wanda pick Rio. She’s highly considering at least light physical intimidation when Wanda opens her mouth and confidently and calmly says, “Rio.”

    Well, that was easy. Either Wanda is the dumbest person alive for putting Rio and Agatha on the same tribe, or she’s trying to gain favor with Agatha. Or it’s not strategic at all and her gay son also loves Rio. Either way, Agatha is almost embarrassed by how much her whole body relaxes when Rio jogs over to them. Rio, to her credit, just gives Wanda a nod, then Agatha a small smile and a wink.

    When it’s Rio’s turn to pick from New Blood, she slowly eyes the remaining players. Finally, with the careful precision she puts into every choice, she decidedly says, “Nick.”

    Agatha can easily follow Rio’s thought process. Nick is young and enthusiastic, a smile on his face even when his tribe loses. He has a perfect combination of skill they can use in challenges and naïveté they can use behind the scenes. There’s something else about him too, a familiarity that Agatha can’t quite put her finger on.

    But it doesn't matter. All Agatha needs to know about her new tribemates is how she can use them to get to the end.

    After their tribe is rounded out with Harold and his dumb mustache, plus two men both named John, Jeff immediately ushers them into a reward challenge, one of those relay-race obstacle courses that he gets so hard for. “The first tribe to complete the course will win a barbecue back at camp: burgers, hot dogs, cornbread, all the fixings. Worth playing for?” Everyone, of course, cheers. Jeff would execute them on the spot if they didn’t.

    “Each tribe will be divided into three groups for this one,” Jeff says, “swimming, climbing, and solving a puzzle. I’ll give you a minute to strategize then we’ll get started.”

    “I’ll take the puzzle,” Agatha says at the same time as this Nick kid. Agatha glares at him. Puzzles are her turf.

    “Perfect,” Rio says, pointedly looking at Agatha in a way that says play nice. “Agatha and Nick can tag team the puzzle.”

    Agatha does not tag team.

    “Don’t fuck this up,” Agatha says to Nick, as they position themself to solve the puzzle.

    He just gives her a goofy salute and says, “aye-aye captain.” There goes any hope of dinner tonight.

    The tribes are mostly evenly matched in the swimming and climbing portion, Sakaar winning a slight lead as Rio handily climbs to the top of a rickety structure to chop through a rope and release puzzle pieces. The skull on her bicep flexes as she wields that knife she loves so much. God, she’s a marvel.

    Tragically, Agatha has tasks at hand that aren’t ogling the beautiful creature she is going to defile later tonight. Rio runs the puzzle pieces up to Agatha and Nick and then it’s go time.

    The goal is to try to spell out a phrase with the pieces, which are a variety of shapes and sizes. Agatha’s eyes immediately go to which pieces have clear letters on them, which words can be obviously formed from them. “Eat,” Nick says to her right. He’s clearly doing the same as her, having already found two pieces that spell the word eat, placing them on the table. Not bad, Agatha has to admit. Maybe the kid’s not going completely to drag her down.

    “There’s a GHT here too,” Agatha says, placing the letters on the board. “Night, fight, might, could be one of those.”

    “Nice,” Nick says, “I’ll look for vowels.”

    So Nick looks for vowels while Agatha singles out the other letters, placing them in a pile. She glances over at the other team that has barely started. Chumps.

    “There’s an OOD,” Nick is muttering. “Food?”

    “There’s no F,” Agatha says. “Try G.”

    “Right,” Nick says. “Eat good?”

    “It’s an ING,” Agatha says, eying Nick’s vowels, “eating Good.” “Amazing,” Nick says, grinning. “Damn, you’re good at this.” “Tell me something I don’t know,” Agatha says, but she can’t help grinning too. Everyone else on this season has eyed her up with something like suspicion, but this kid is looking at her with nothing but awe and camaraderie. It’s stupid of him, but Agatha finds she doesn't hate it. “Tonight,” he’s saying, looking back. “Eating good tonight.” “We are,” Agatha says, arranging the five final letters, giddy. “That’s the rest of it.”

    “Oh, hell yeah,” Nick says, hands quickly moving to fill the rest of the puzzle, as Agatha lines up the letters.

    He doesn't take her pieces, doesn’t aggressively go so fast he ruins it, just works around her seamlessly until—

    “Jeff!” Nick calls. “We’ve got it.”

    “Agatha and Nick think they have it,” Jeff says as he jogs over. “It would be a quick finish if they do and”—he reads off the puzzle—“We are eating good tonight! That is correct! Sakaar wins reward!”

    “Yes!” Nick calls childishly, pumping a fist and then holding up a hand for Agatha to high-five. Agatha can’t remember the last time she has done something so juvenile as a high five, but her hand, almost of its we own accord, meets Nick’s in the air. “You rocked it!”

    “Always do,” Agatha says, grinning. “And hey, you weren’t terrible yourself.”

    He smiles at her, again with no wariness, no caution. Just an excited kid. Oh, this game is going to ruin him.

    Day 11 “My son was telling me that this beach on Fiji is where they’re going to film all the themed seasons from now on,” Wanda is saying. If Agatha has learned anything in the last 24 hours of being on a tribe with Wanda is that this bitch won’t shut up about her damn kids. “That’s so interesting,” Agatha says, shoving rice into her mouth. “Gay son or straight son?”

    Rio, eating rice at Agatha's side, snickers a little. At least someone appreciates Agatha’s humor.

    “Like, I said, they’re 13,” Wanda says. She eyes the constant cameras. “Not that I wouldn’t—I love my sons, no matter what. Whatever they, um, whoever they love, is definitely…” One of Agatha’s favorite activities in the world is to fluster straight people, and this woman is so damn easy. “I mean, what kind of mother wouldn’t, you know?”

    “Oh, you’d be surprised.”

    Agatha is shocked the words aren’t coming out of her own mouth, but it’s young, sweet Nick that said it, not even looking at Wanda, but down at his rice. In the 24 hours Agatha has known him, Nick has had a constant grin on his face, displaying his slightly too far apart teeth. But he’s very much not smiling right now, looking down grimly with a mix of embarrassment and old resentment.

    And oh, Agatha knows that look. She recognizes it from the mirror when she was Nick’s tender age, when the pain was still raw. She remembers that first year on her own in Boston, when people would bring up their mothers in casual conversation and Agatha couldn’t look them in the eye, only stew in jealousy of how lucky they were to have the unconditional love that all children allegedly are promised by the one who birthed them. It’s dulled over the years, but never fully disappeared, always flaring up in those moments she has to interact with said hag who birthed her. But time has helped. Time and Rio Vidal sitting in a Ford F-150 outside of a Dunkin’ Donuts, earnestly making a promise to poison Agatha’s mother. Rio who, right now, lifts a hand to the small of Agatha’s back, a type of comfort that is so innate to Rio. Nothing too bold, no sweet words or false promises, just a simple touch of I’m here.

    Agatha looks across the fire to Nick, still casting his eyes downward. Agatha wonders if he has anyone to press a hand to his back when things get too dark.

    “I’m gonna go for a swim,” he announces abruptly. “Thanks for the rice.” Then he’s off down the beach.

    “Was it something I said?” Wanda asks.

    “Probably,” Agatha says. Rio nudges her. “Kidding! You know how teen boys are. Hormones. Frontal lobe still doing its thing.” Agatha stands up, stretches. “Well, this has been a lovely dinner.” She pats her stomach. “Gotta walk off all this rice.”

    Rio shoots Agatha a look of you good?

    Agatha nods. She’s good. She can't say the same for the boy, though. Agatha isn’t sure what exactly compels her to get up and follow Nick down the beach, but here she is, her own footprints joining his in the sand. She remembers some inane bible story her mom’s group used to share about two sets of footprints in the sand when God walked besides you during good times, but only one set when God was carrying you during hard times. Agatha once made a joke that God should maybe spend less time on the beach and more time in war zones, but Evanora wasn’t too fond of that one. Nick’s footprints lead Agatha around the bend from their camp, to an outcropping of rock that is far enough away that no one can spot them. Agatha climbs up on the rocks to see that they have created a cove of sorts, a swimming hole of calm water amidst the choppy ocean.

    This is only Agatha’s second day at this new beach; it was New Blood’s before. She wonders how many times Nick has come out here to be alone, why such a cheery and excitable guy would need a place to escape the company of others.

    He’s in the water now, swimming back and forth in the cove. Agatha stands on the rocks and watches him for a few seconds, before realizing how fucking weird she looks. Why did she come out here anyway? The kid clearly wants to be by himself. And what is she going to do, provide platitudes to comfort a stranger? That’s not exactly her forte. So what if Nick accidentally projected his mommy issues to the whole camp? Agatha has met plenty of people with mommy issues in her day, and none of them inspired her to vaguely stalk someone. God, who does she think she is, the mommy-issues-whisperer? Fuck that.

    She turns on her heel to leave the poor kid the hell alone when— “Agatha!”

    Shit. Agatha turns to see Nick pulling himself out of the water, shaking his shaggy hair like a dog.

    “Hey,” Agatha says lamely. “Just scouting out for good hiding spots. This one might make the shortlist, but I’m still partial to that big hollowed out tree trunk in the jungle.”

    Nick chuckles, using his t-shirt to towel off his hair. He’s one of the only guys on this show who doesn’t insist on being shirtless around camp and in the challenges, and he has the farmer’s tan to prove it.

    “Oh,” he says, smile back on his face, “so you aren’t here because I freaked out at camp at the even the idea of a supportive mother?” “Oh that?” Agatha says, waving a hand. “That shit’s none of my business.” Nick laughs a little. He squeezes his t-shirt out, before putting it back on. In the brief second before he does, Agatha’s eyes fall to his chest, to the faint scar lines there. Just like the puzzles they’re both so good at, the pieces connect. She quickly looks back up into Nick’s face. He’s still smiling, but he catches her eye purposefully, knowingly, before looking away. Nick settles on the rocks, letting the evening Fijian sun dry him off. He doesn’t tell Agatha to leave but he doesn’t ask her to stay. Agatha stands awkwardly for a few seconds, but eventually sits next to him. Not too close, but nearby.

    She draws her knees up to her chest, watches the beginnings of the sunset, the sky slowly turning from blue into pinks and oranges.

    The blue is almost fully gone before Agatha finally speaks, trying to craft her words into something that maybe this kid would want to hear. “My very first memory was of my mom yelling at me,” she says, staring out into the sun. “I was maybe two years old, and had broken into the cupboard where she kept the sweets. There was one of those child locks on it, but I had figured out how to break it. She found me covered in chocolate and screamed at me. She said I was too curious for my own good. “She always says that. Too curious. She used to make me attend this horrendous bible group she hosted with her equally miserable friends, and I asked too many questions. Once I asked why Abraham killing his son was seen as a good thing and she made me sit outside in a New England winter, hoping to freeze the curiosity out of me. In high school, she found out I was sneaking around with Lisa Bronson after class, and she wouldn’t let me even talk to Lisa anymore. She said I was too curious for my own good, I wanted too much, I wanted to know things that girls like me weren’t meant to know.

    “But after I left that fucking house, after I turned 18 and escaped by the skin of my teeth, I realized that curiousity is what kept me alive. When you figure out how things work, it’s easy to figure out how you can make them work to your advantage. There are a lot of shitty things about me, kid, but one of the best things about me is that I’m curious. And you”—Agatha finally turns to the boy, who is watching her with intense brown eyes—“you have it too, right? An urge to know. That’s why you’re so good at puzzles. That’s why know yourself, at what? Nineteen? Far before most people do. Don’t let anyone stop you from wanting to know more, from questioning the world, from being curious.     “Yeah, my mom tried pretty hard,” he says, voice cracking. “I have two older brothers and ever since I was little, she kept talking about how thrilled she was to finally have a daughter. That it wasn’t just her and a house full of boys anymore. But then I started growing up, figuring out that maybe it was just her and a house full of boys. She kept—every time I tried to tell her, she would say that it’s just because of my dad and my brothers, that I just wasn’t in touch with my feminine side or whatever. She would put me in horrible dresses to go to horrible pageants and all that bullshit again and again like that would magically make me what she wanted. She kept trying and trying and trying to make me into her daughter, until one day my dad just told her to stop.”

    Nick lets out a deep sigh, far too weary for a boy his age. “You know how most kids blame themselves for their parents’ divorce? And then the parents say ‘oh no honey, it’s not your fault?’ Yeah, when my parents got divorced, it was a hundred perfect my fault, because my dad believed me and listened to me and took me to appointments and my mom was so angry at him for supporting me that their marriage fell apart. She hated me more than she loved him, I guess.”

    He says it so matter-of-factly that something big and hot and angry flares in Agatha, flint striking a knife. Her hand flexes in her lap, desperate to either reach out to him or to reach across space and time to punch this woman in throat.

    Nick puts his head in his hands. Groans. “Fuck! I didn’t come onto Survivor to trauma dump, I didn’t—there was just something about what Wanda said. ‘What kind of mother wouldn’t?’ Mine! It’s just—I know I’m still so fucking lucky. I know that so many trans kids don’t even have one parent who supports them, but hearing something like that just makes me feel so… Like, why couldn’t I get a mom like Wanda or… you or…”

    “Me?” Agatha interrupts, laughing a little. “Listen, your mom seems like a bitch supreme, but you don’t have to say I would be a good mom.” “What do you mean?” Nick asks, blinking innocently up at her. “You just gave that whole follow your curiosity speech, it was really beautiful. I would have—I would have loved something like that growing up. My dad… my dad is a great guy, the best, he saved my life, but he doesn't really know how to talk about it. It would have been nice to have someone around who is good with words like you, who wasn’t, you know, an English teacher or a therapist.”

    And then, maybe because Agatha can’t bear another second of Nick’s earnestness, or his pain, or the shock still radiating through her that anyone would ever think that she would be a good parent, Agatha changes tactics, shifting her face into a pout.

    “Do you want me to be your Survivor mommy?” she asks in a baby voice. It does the trick. Nick cracks up, throwing his head back and laughing so hard that his unshed tears fall down his cheeks, to the stubble that is trying desperately to grow there.

    “God, don’t ever say that again,” he says, wheezing.

    Agatha grins. “Aw, does widdle Nicky not want a Survivor mommy?” He chokes laughing. “Please, Agatha, I beg.”

    She exaggeratedly frowns at him and he starts laughing again, unable to help himself. God, it feels good to make this sweet kid laugh after he’s been through so much. To be the person to make him smile.

    “Oh my god,” Nick says, wiping his eyes. “I needed that.” He looks at her intensely again, even as he’s still smiling. “People out here are too harsh on you, you know. You really aren’t the villain they say you are.” Agatha scoffs. “Please, Nicholas, I have a reputation to uphold.” “I’m serious,” Nick says. “When I watched your season, I wasn’t struck by the idols and the blindsides and all that, I was struck by your loyalty.” “What are you talking about?” Agatha says. “My whole thing was that I screwed everyone over. That’s how I got to the end.”

    “Not everyone,” Nick says pointedly. “You never sent Rio home, even though there were a million times you could have. Watching you two play together was… I can’t believe I get to be on a season with both of you.” Agatha swallows. So damn earnest.

    “Yeah, well, she’s…. Rio’s something else. She’s not just some random Survivor player, she’s…” Agatha looks off into the sunset again. She can only afford to be genuine so many times in a few short minutes. And if she starts talking about Rio, she might never stop.

    “Wow,” Nick says. Then with a devilish grin, he puts on his own baby voice and says, “can she be my Survivor Daddy?”

    “Fuck off,” Agatha says, but it’s her turn to laugh now, out of control with it.

    Nick catches her eyes and smiles, proud of himself for his dumb joke. A surge of affection hits Agatha in the chest, harder than the waves crashing on the rocks beneath them; a fondness for this kid who has been through more in less than two decades than most people have been through in their entire lives, but is still somehow laughing through it, full of an energy and joy and that Agatha was never able to conjure at his age. It’s irrational, it’s stupid. She’s only known this kid for a day and they’re on Survivor for fuck’s sake, but somehow, sitting on these rocks, Agatha knows she will do whatever it takes so nothing bad will happen to him out here.

    Day 12 “Look at this haul!” Nick says gleefully, dumping a bag of fish gracelessly into their camp.

    Agatha looks up from her busy afternoon of gleefully doing nothing after they won the immunity challenge to see the only two people on this island she gives a shit about.

    “The kid’s a whiz,” Rio says from behind Nick.

    She’s all wet and salty from the ocean, sunlight highlighting the glow of her skin. Agatha looks away before she does something stupid. “Rio’s amazing,” Nick says, with genuine awe. “Like, she can just catch them with her bare hands.”

    “She is very good with her hands,” Agatha agrees.

    Rio laughs, shooting Agatha a pointed look before clapping Nick on the back.

    “Nah, you did most of it,” Rio says. “You should see this kid with a Hawaiian sling, he’s out of this world.”

    Nick grins up at Rio like she’s the coolest person in the world. She probably is. She smiles back at him, both of them with little gaps in their teeth and salt in their hair.

    Once again, Agatha has to look away.

    “What do you think of Nick?” Agatha asks Rio later that night. They’re sitting in the hollowed out tree in the jungle, where Rio has laid palm fronds on the ground so it’s comfortable for them to stretch out on, to look at the stars, or fuck each other silly, depending on the mood. “Nick?” Rio says, brow furrowing. “I like Nick.”

    “I like Nick too,” Agatha says. She reaches up, smooths the lines from Rio’s forehead. “Why are you frowning?”

    Rio leans into Agatha’s touch like she always does, like it’s instinct. “Because I don’t want to vote off Nick,” Rio says with a wry chuckle. “Me neither,” Agatha says, stomach clenching.

    “Well, you just said his name, so I figured…”

    Agatha laughs, relieved. “I said his name because I like him, not because I want to vote him out.”

    “Oh!” Rio says, clear surprise on her face. “Cool. I guess I’m just not used to you…”

    “Enjoying the company of others?”

    “Who aren’t me,” Rio says, laughing. “But Nick’s great, I get it.” “What do you think—” God, Agatha’s throat is dry, her palms are sweaty, she can’t believe she’s actually nervous about this. How embarrassing. “What do you think about adding him to our alliance?”

    There’s silence for a second, besides the chirping of the bugs. “Wow,” Rio finally says. “Until the end?”

    “I mean, if we can,” Agatha says quickly. Maybe if she talks fast enough, Rio won’t pick up on how much she cares. “I don’t know if they’ll do a top three or a top two this year, but it could be… I would. He’s been through so much, Rio, more than—I don’t want Survivor to be just one more thing that makes his life harder.”

    Before Agatha even finishes her sentence, Rio’s hand is in hers, gently squeezing.

    “Okay,” Rio says softly. “I don’t… I think Nick is a great kid and I would genuinely love to bring him in and see how far we can take him, but our plan doesn’t exactly work with three people. We can adjust, but it might… I don’t want to promise him something we can’t deliver on.” The plan they came up with back in Vermont is complex and layered, yet deceptively simple. It hinges on the merge, where Agatha will do something big and despicable at tribal and Rio will loudly and publicly turn on her, and join the other alliance. Then Rio will feed her information while also building a case with the others that if they take Agatha to the end, they will win. And once they get to the end, then comes the reveal. How the two of them have been in it together from the start: the alliance, the secrecy, the marriage. No one can say no to voting for that.

    But that plan was concocted for only two people.

    “We can adjust!” Agatha says, to convince herself just as much as Rio. “Of course,” Rio says gently. “But if people find out he’s allies with us, with you, they might try to vote him out down the line. Someone as likable as him will definitely get a target on his back.”

    “We’ll protect him,” Agatha says simply.

    “It might not be that—”

    “Rio.” Agatha places her hands on either side of Rio’s face. “Do you want to bring him in or not?”

    Rio takes a deep breath in. She’s beautiful everywhere, but she’s maybe the most out beautiful out here, skin vibrant from the sun and saltwater, eyes brought alive by the jungle.

    “Yeah,” Rio says softly. “Let’s bring him in. Even if it won’t—” Agatha leans forward and kisses her. In their tree trunk in Fiji with no one else, Agatha kisses her wife senseless, mostly because she wants to, mostly because she loves her.

    But a little bit so she won’t finish her sentence.

    Chapter End Notes for all you survivor heads, I haven’t gotten to zeke’s season yet, but have read a lot about it and he was def an inspo for survivor nick. also this piece which is lovely!!

    Chapter 15

    Chapter Notes there was a time when I was able to write chapters of a normal length. there was a time when I posted every wednesday. but that time is long gone, it’s friday night and here are almost 17k words 😈 graphics by nybagels as per use!!

    See the end of the chapter for more notes

    Day 13 “Sakaar wins reward!” Jeff yells, “another incredible turn from Agatha and Nick on the puzzle, you can’t stop these two.”

    This time, Agatha is the one who offers her hand up for a high five. Nick grins as he slaps her hand, free and easy.

    “Your tribe has won a picnic by one of the majestic waterfalls of Fiji,” Jeff is saying. “Sandwiches, chicken wings, potato salad, Mountain Dew, you’ve got it all. Go out and enjoy it!”

    Agatha, despite the deep seated instinct never to do what Jeff Probst tells her, does go out and enjoy it. Reward challenges have historically been where she gets to work planting seeds for future false alliances, gathering any intel she can use, combing through each piece of food for idol hints. But today, something about the bright sun, the glow of Nick’s smile, the way Rio jostles Agatha’s side on the way to the boat, makes Agatha put strategy to the back of her mind just for one afternoon.

    She indulges. In the food and the company. She doesn’t really care about Harold, Wanda and the Johns, but she does care about Rio drinking Mountain Dew like she’s the thirstiest woman alive, wiping her mouth and grinning afterwards. She cares about Nick generously offering the sandwich basket around to everyone else before eating his own.

    After they eat, everyone makes their way to the swimming hole beneath the waterfall, not caring that they ate far less than 30 minutes ago, simply desperate to have cool water on their hot skin.

    Eventually, Wanda and Harold and the Johns disappear further downriver, probably plotting who they think the next elimination will be (it will be Blonde John, Agatha and Rio have already decided, but let them think what they want to think).

    It leaves Rio and Agatha and Nick alone in the swimming hole, Nick and Rio taking turns seeing how long they can put their heads under the waterfall until the pressure becomes too much and they have to pull away, laughing. Agatha dives deeper under the water when the sight of the two of them becomes too bright to look at.

    When she comes up, both Nick and Rio are watching her. Rio has a grin on her face, toe brushing Agatha’s leg under the water.

    “Was there something you wanted to ask Nick?” Rio says. She looks around for the others. “Now seems the perfect time to… have a conversation.” “Is this The Talk?” Nick asks, grinning. “I know I look young, but trust me, I'm very aware of how the mechanics work.”

    “So when two Survivor contestants love each other very much…” Rio says dryly.

    Nick laughs. “They make a baby Survivor contestant?”

    “In a way, yes,” Rio says.

    Agatha rolls her eyes. “Jesus, you’re both making this weird. Nick, how do you feel about joining our alliance?”

    As soon as she says it, Agatha fights the urge to swim underwater again and never come up.

    But she stands her ground, somehow resists the urge to turn away, and is rewarded with one of those gap-toothed Nick grins, wide and unburdened, though he has so much to be burdened by.

    “For real?” he asks. “Not one of your schemes?”

    Agatha splashes him. “Please, I’d never waste a scheme on you.” “And she wouldn’t be so nervous about it,” Rio adds.

    Agatha splashes her.

    Nick laughs, the sound echoing off the rocks.

    “In that case, I’d love to join your alliance. Obviously. My two gay Survivor moms.”

    “And our widdle Nicky,” Agatha coos in a joking voice.

    It’s easier to joke, easier to laugh and splash around in the water like all three of them are Nick’s age then to actually think about what this means. To think that Agatha started playing this game for herself and herself only but now there are two people who have gotten in the way of that, two people that tug at Agatha’s chest in a way she never thought anyone would again.

    Day 14 It’s nice, is the problem. Almost unbearably nice to wake up in Rio’s arms like she has been doing for the last year and then go on a long walk with Nick, swapping stories back and forth, talking out in the sun so long that the tips of their ears and noses burn with it.

    It’s all too nice. Too nice for reality TV. Too nice for the first 45 years of Agatha’s life.

    Day 15 Don’t get Agatha wrong—these touchy feely good vibes have not stopped her from playing the game. She still came out here for one distinctive purpose. To win. And her newest alliance member can help with that. After their tribe suffers an embarrassing immunity loss, Agatha regroups with Nick and Rio by the well, the three of them huddled together by for a quick minute of strategy talk before they will inevitably be interrupted. “So John, John, Wanda and Harold are planning to vote Agatha,” Rio is saying. “But we can easily get one of them gone.”

    “Blonde John is the biggest threat,” Agatha says, “let’s go for him.” “But keep an eye on Wanda for the future,” Rio warns. “She’s smarter than she looks.”

    “Wait, are you using an idol on John?” Nick asks.

    “Oh, sweet Nicky,” Agatha says, laughing. “We don’t need an idol. We have you now.”

    “I’m just one vote,” Nick says.

    “But you’re young and innocent and people like you,” Agatha says. “And they don’t know how deep you’re in with us. For all Harold and Bald John know, you’re aligned with Wanda and Blonde John to take out Old Blood.” “But I’m not,” Nicky says.

    “But you could be,” Agatha says. “And you could have had a change of heart. You could tell Bald John that Blonde John is planning on voting for him after getting me out, and the vote on me is just a ruse to thin the Old Blood numbers. You could say ‘hey, let’s work together to get someone young and strong like Blonde John out before bothering with someone little ol’ Agatha.’”

    “God, you’re good, baby,” Rio says, voice low.

    “Not in front of the child,” Agatha says, hitting Rio in the arm. Nick just looks back and forth between the two of them.

    “Okay,” he says slowly. “I’m not that good of a liar, but I can try it. Blonde John is kind of an asshole anyway, so I’d rather get him out than you.” “That’s the spirit!” Agatha says. “Go get ‘em!”

    Nick looks like he’s about to say something else, some hesitation in his face, but then there’s a rustle in the trees, and the three of them disperse, Nick down one path and Agatha and Rio down the other.

    “Do you think he’ll do it?” Rio asks softly once they are a safe distance from the well.

    “You heard him,” Agatha says, “Blonde John is kind of an asshole.” “Sure,” Rio says. “But what about when he has to blindside someone he actually likes?”

    “He’ll do it,” Agatha says. She swats a fly away from her face, wishing she could do the same with this conversation. She understands why all those hack comedians go on and on about their nagging wives. “Have a little faith. Why would anyone go on Survivor if they can’t lie?” But even as the words leave her mouth, Agatha tastes the bitter falseness in them. People go on Survivor for a million dollars and for something else. And Agatha knows Nick’s second reason has nothing to do with blindsiding and lying and deceiving. Nick, a kid who had to watch his mom choose her hatefulness over her own child. Agatha would bet the million dollars she will be winning that Nick’s other reason has a lot more to do with proving to the world and his mother what kind of man he is than any form of gameplay.

    Even so, Nick comes up to Agatha minutes before tribal, and gives her a not so covert thumbs up.

    “Bald John and Harold are in for Blonde John,” he whispers. “Good kid,” Agatha says.

    “I’m sweating so much,” he says with a little laugh.

    “It’s over a hundred degrees out.”

    “It’s not that,” Nick says, still jovial. “It’s just… how do you lie like that all the time? My stomach was in knots.”

    She looks at him for a minute, his bright brown eyes so trusting even now. She reaches out and pats him on the cheek.

    “I started lying like that long before Survivor, kid. Hopefully you won’t ever have to out there.”

    “Even on Survivor,” Nick says. “It’s a lot.”

    “The game’s not real life, Nicky,” Agatha says softly. “Don’t forget that.”

    Day 16 It’s hard to remember sometimes though, that it’s not real life. That it’s not just her and Rio and Nicky against the world, that he’s not going to go home with them to Vermont.

    Day 17 It’s hard to remember—when there’s Nicky, holding a fake birthday cake, the flame that Rio lit highlighting his grin—that they are almost halfway through this game, that there might only be a top two, that the three of them can’t last forever, that someday, their little bubble will burst and Jeff will say—

    Day 18 “The tribe has spoken,” Jeff says, snuffing Harold’s torch. “What the heck, guys!” Harold asks, throwing up his hands. Good riddance; Agatha was getting sick of his dumb mustache anyway.

    Day 19 ”Does everything have to be a blindside?” Nicky asks.

    The two of them are floating on their backs in his cove, enjoying a moment of peace away from camp after Harold’s elimination.

    “Yes,” Agatha answers simply.

    Nicky laughs freely. “That’s not true. You can get enough people in on a vote without lying to them.”

    “Not in my experience,” Agatha says.

    “That’s because you’ve never done anything else.”

    “People will turn on you even if they like you, even if you used to be allies. That’s how this game works. So it’s better to turn on them first, before they get the chance.”

    “Hmm,” Nicky says, annoyingly perceptive. “Still talking Survivor here?” “Always,” Agatha says, leaning her head up just enough to wink at him. “And you have to get your head in the game, Nicky. Merge is coming up. If you think the blindsides are bad now—”

    Day 21 “Jesus, Agatha, the man is blind,” Todd is saying, once their newly merged tribe makes it back to camp after Matt’s masterful elimination, if Agatha does say so herself.

    “It’s not called a blindside for nothing, Ted,” Agatha says cheerfully. “Hey, we have any wine left from the merge feast?”

    “His name is Todd,” Rio corrects. She crosses her arms over her chest and scowls. Agatha can still see the smile in her eyes, though. “And listen, Agatha, a line was crossed, even for you.”

    Rio is no Agatha, but she’s not too bad at this whole acting thing. Agatha is immensely proud.

    “Yeah, Agatha,” Wanda chimes in, “maybe a fake idol for Matt was a bit too far.”

    “Okay, there’s no need to gang up on her,” Nick adds. “It’s been a long day, maybe we just get some rest and talk about this in the morning.” Agatha could throttle him. The last thing Nicky needs to do is defend her. That’s just putting a target on his back.

    “Glad to see the 19-year-old is the most mature among you,” she says. “You all should take a good hard look in the mirror.”

    “There aren’t mirrors out here,” Kate points out.

    Agatha rolls her eyes. “What a warm welcome to the new tribe.” Then, she stomps off toward the woods, managing to hide her smile until she’s a few paces away from everyone. God, this is fun.

    “Agatha!” It’s Rio, of course, storming after her like they’ve been planning. “Don’t come after me,” Agatha yells over her shoulder, very aware that they have an audience.

    “Seriously?” Rio says.

    Agatha finally turns around, which is almost a mistake, because as soon as she does, Rio’s eyes spark with mischief and Agatha’s body wants to leap into her arms and kiss her and ruin their whole damn plan. “It’s clear you're with them and against me,” Agatha says, conjuring tears to her eyes.

    “Maybe I am!” Rio says with her mouth while her eyes tell a different story. Her eyes say meet me in the woods after everyone falls asleep. “Go on,” Agatha says. “Join them.”

    “I will,” Rio says, crossing her arms and biting her lip so as not to smile. “I’ve played a season and a half with you, that’s more than enough.” “I couldn’t agree more,” Agatha spits.

    “Great!” Rio says.

    “Wonderful,” Agatha snarls, waving a hand at her. “Bye bye now!” “Jesus, Agatha,” Rio mutters, shaking her head and walking away.

    Day 22 “Jesus, Agatha,” Rio moans from under Agatha. “God, that feels so good.” Agatha smiles into Rio’s mouth, pressing slow and deep into Rio. She loves her like this, so pliant, so vocal, overflowing with her need. “You were so good yesterday, baby,” Agatha whispers in her ear, feels Rio clench around her fingers in response. Christ, she’s so beautifully easy. “Being so mad at me, playing our game so well.”

    They’ve managed to sneak away from everyone while half the tribe is on a quest for coconuts and the other half is napping. Agatha has Rio pressed on to the ground of their tree trunk, watching her sweat and pant under Agatha’s hand.

    “I wanted to do this last night,” Agatha tells her, “you were so mad, it was breathtaking.”

    Rio has abandoned the concept of words and is now making only sharp breathy sounds, which means she’s close.

    Agatha can’t believe it sometimes. This gorgeous creature, so strong and capable, turned to mush just from Agatha’s hand, just from Agatha’s words. It makes Agatha feel invincible.

    “Just like that, sweetheart,” Agatha whispers softly in Rio’s ear as she comes under Agatha’s hand, the gentleness coming out unbidden when faced with Rio so open beneath her.

    It takes a while for Rio to be able to talk again, Agatha notes with pride. She lies down beside Rio, listens to her heavy breaths echoing through the jungle.

    “It’s pretty fun pretending mad at you,” Rio finally pants, “especially when this is part of it.”

    She grins, gap toothed and messy and Agatha kisses the smile off her face. “Any intel?” Agatha asks.

    “Such a romantic,” Rio says, laughing. “But, yes, the plan is if neither of you win immunity, they’re splitting the vote between you and Nick next tribal.”

    Agatha shoots up, relaxation gone.

    “And you waited until now to tell me?”

    Rio sits up, with her typical post-coital slowness that Agatha normally finds charming, but right now is frustratingly unrushed.

    “In my defense,” Rio says, “you kind of jumped me as soon as I got out here.”

    Rio grins. This time Agatha does not want to kiss it off of her. “I’m serious,” Agatha snaps. Rio’s grin fades. “We need a plan.” “We have an idol,” Rio says. “And me.”

    “What if it’s 3-3-3,” Agatha presses. “Even if you vote with us and we play an idol that’s three on either Nick or me and then they revote and we’re fucked.”

    There’s a panicked edge to her voice that Agatha hates. Rio, of course, spots it immieditely.

    “Hey,” she says softly. “Hey, sweetheart, it’s okay. I’ll see if we can try to split the vote so me switching makes it 4-2 and then we give the idol to whichever one of you gets four.”

    Agatha shakes her head. She feels bile rising in her throat. “They won’t trust you that much. They’ll do 3-3 and have you be a wild card. We need— we need another idol or for Nicky to join their alliance to save himself or —”

    “Agatha,” Rio’s voice is calm, sturdy. She puts a hand up to Agatha’s cheek, warm and firm. “We’ll be okay. We’ll get another idol. We’ll talk to Nicky. It’s us. We can survive this tribal.”

    And Agatha almost believes her. If anyone can find an idol it’s the two of them. If anyone can spread the right lies, it’s the two of them. “But,” Rio says and all of Agatha’s calmness flies right out the window. Rio looks down for a second. “Like I said, I don’t know how long all three of us can last as a unit. Not how we play this game. Not when Nick is so damn honest. We can try our best, but it might not… we might not be the three who make it to the end.”

    Agatha tears her head from Rio’s grip. “Well, not with that attitude,” she snaps. “We made it to the end last time, we can do it again.” “You made it to the end last time,” Rio clarifies. “Sure, this time, people might take you to the end because they won’t think you’ll get votes, but Nick… Nick is smart and strong and likeable and has firmly placed himself with you. That’s a threat, baby. No lies we tell can erase that.” It makes sense. Of course it does. Rio is perceptive and logical and knows how this game works. That’s why she was so easy to play with last year, was so easy to fall for. But right now, Agatha kind of wants to strangle her for it.

    “Then you better find that fucking idol,” Agatha says, voice hard. Rio, undeterred, simply nods. “We will,” she says confidently. “We will be fine this tribal, I promise. But I can’t promise what happens next time.” “Well, you should,” Agatha snaps.

    Rio laughs a little.

    “Can we go back to, like, ten minutes ago?” she asks.

    There is nothing Agatha would like to do more than go back to ten minutes ago. To before Rio told her they were splitting the vote, before this fear bloomed in Agatha’s chest for some fucking kid. Some kid who deserves better than an early exit, who deserves to prove to any bigoted fucks watching that he can do this.

    “No,” Agatha says simply. Rio’s face falls and Agatha leans forward and quickly kisses her on the mouth. “We have an idol to find.”

    Day 23 ”The next item up for auction is…” Jeff says, dragging it out. Agatha leans forward. Her stomach grumbles, eying the burger that Nick paid $300 for and the pizza Kate spent all of her $500 on. But Agatha is here for something more important than food. “A hint to a hidden immunity idol.” “Five hundred dollars!” Rio calls, before anyone else can even react. Oh, Agatha loves her. Spending all her money at the Survivor auction just for an advantage. Just for an idol. Just to keep their boy here. “Alright,” Jeff says, chuckling, “the hint goes to Rio. And there goes any chance of food for her.”

    Rio shrugs. “Worth it.”

    Day 24 “They should really make these things harder to find,” Rio says, slipping the idol into Nick’s hand.

    “Maybe you’re just that good,” Nick says, sweetly. “And thanks, you guys, you really don’t need to waste your idols on me.”

    “Hey!” Agatha says sharply. “It’s not a waste. We need you here.” Nick smiles at her, gap toothed and genuine. “Thanks, Agatha,” he says, all soft. “And Rio, duh, for the idol.”

    “Don’t say it was from me,” Rio cautions. “I’m telling everyone I can’t find it, and that Agatha pulled one over on me. But you two are safe tonight, and that’s what matters.”

    Then with a quick grin, she’s gone down the beach, back to her supposed alliance.

    “Vote for Sarah,” Agatha tells Nick. “We’ll get three votes on her and then we can blame Rio’s vote on Kate.”

    “Right,” Nick says. “I—couldn’t Rio just vote with everyone else? I don’t know why we need to plant these rumors about Kate. I like Kate.” He looks nervous, not looking at Agatha, but instead at the patterns he’s drawing in the sand with his toes.

    “We have to think long term,” Agatha says, “if they think someone defected, that’s who they’ll vote out next. Tomorrow, you tell them that Kate approached you about getting more Old Blood people out, then she’ll become the number one target instead of you.”

    “Do I have to?” Nicky asks, like he’s being asked to clean his room. “Yes, you do,” she says firmly, but gently. “People want to vote for you because you’re young and strong and charming. Kate’s all of those things too, but she’s in with them. If they even suspect her slipping a little bit, they will vote her out instead of you. Don’t you want to stay?” Nicky finally looks up at her, still smiling, thank god. “Of course I want to stay,” he says. “But it’s not the end of the world if I’m voted out, you know? At least I’ll have made the jury.”

    “Don’t talk like that,” Agatha snaps. Then she levels her voice. “All I mean is, you deserve better than just the jury, you deserve top three.” “And what happens after top three, Agatha?” Nicky asks, not accusatory, but gently. “I’ll understand if you and Rio vote me out, I will.” “We won’t do that, I promise,” Agatha says. It comes out of her mouth unbidden, but she means it. There’s no way she is writing down the boy’s name and she knows Rio won’t either. “Let’s just focus on getting through tonight, okay?”

    “I thought you told me to think long term,” Nicky says with a raise of his eyebrows.

    “Shut up,” Agatha says eloquently. “Just play your damn idol.”

    Day 25 “Okay, I gotta admit,” Kate says, “the double idol play was pretty sick.” Nicky laughs. “That’s always the goal, right?”

    “It’s my personal goal, yeah,” Kate says, laughing with him, “to look cool on TV.”

    “Those two are getting kind of cozy, aren’t they?” Agatha whispers to Wanda, across the fire from Nick and Kate.

    Wanda just shrugs. “They’re just two kids, they have a lot in common. Unless you’re suggesting…”

    “Oh, I’m not suggesting anything,” Agatha says, “only that if I were in an alliance with Kate, I’d be nervous.”

    Wanda laughs. “Sure, Agatha.”

    Day 26 “What if we did Wanda instead of Kate tomorrow?” Rio asks when they get a moment alone, whispering by tree mail. “Kate’s good at challenges, sure, but Wanda’s got all those guys wrapped around her finger every time she talks about her family. She’s leading that alliance and they aren’t even onto her.”

    Agatha can’t help it, she laughs. Wanda is the least of her worries. “Kate won immunity in the last challenge,” Agatha says, “If we get her tomorrow, it opens the door for us and Nicky in challenges. Plus the pieces are already moving on Kate.”

    “I know, I know,” Rio says. She’s looked stressed the last couple days. Like she did back in Vermont last winter when she had to file all her taxes on her Survivor stipend, plus the new truck, plus her plot of land, plus all of Agatha’s.

    “Just don’t count Wanda out,” Rio continues. “She’s smart. Like legitimately smart.”

    “Please,” Agatha says. “She’s nothing. None of these people are. We get Kate gone, then we can talk about it. For now—”

    “I know,” Rio says, “we have to keep Nick in. I’m doing everything I can, but—”

    “Good,” Agatha says, cutting her off with a kiss, which Rio of course reciprocates, dropping her train of thought, dropping the furrow from her brow. So predictable. Agatha pulls back with a smile. “Now, let's see what this dumb reward challenge is.”

    The dumb reward challenge involves digging up a chest from the dirt. Which means the reward challenge is Rio’s. Which means that Rio wins a full feast from Outback Steakhouse. Which means Rio gets to take someone on the reward with her, gets to strengthen them up. Which means—

    Day 27 “Nick wins immunity!” Jeff calls, and relief floods through Agatha, “that steak dinner yesterday sure helped him destroy this challenge!”

    Day 28 “Thank you,” Agatha murmurs into Rio’s mouth in the wee hours of the morning, “thank you, thank you, baby. For saving him.”

    “I don’t know how much longer I can do this,” Rio says, her back hitting a tree. “Now that Kate’s gone, they’ll be gunning for Nick. He’s too damn likeable.”

    “Stop talking,” Agatha growls. She bites down on Rio’s ear. “We can do anything, right? Send anyone home?”

    She runs her nails over Rio’s stomach, teasing the top of her shorts. “I—I don’t know if, oh my god, Agatha, please, I need—”

    Day 29 “I need you to start being an asshole,” Agatha says to Nick as they walk toward the reward challenge.

    Nick laughs, long and loud.

    “I’m not going to start being an asshole,” he says.

    “Come on,” Agatha says, “I do and it’s working out great for me.” “But you’re not an asshole,” Nick says like it’s fact. Like he’s known Agatha for 45 years, not 19 days. Like she is someone who is kind at her core like him. “You just pretend to be on Survivor.”

    It’s Agatha’s turn to laugh.

    “Please,” she says. “I’m serious. If you just become a little bit more of a dick, it can do wonders for you. Make people want to take you to the end instead of voting you out.”

    “Nah,” he says, “I can’t do that. I spent over half of my life pretending to be something I’m not, I’m not starting now. Even if it gets me voted out.” He says it like it’s nothing, like it’s easy, to be so sure of oneself so young. “I’m not—” Agatha has always been good with her words. They are her greatest weapon, the best tool she has to get what she wants. But something about this kid’s earnest simplicity makes her unable to find the right ones. “I wouldn’t ask you to—”

    “I know,” Nick says, putting a hand on her shoulder. “I know, Agatha. I just can’t play Survivor like you do. Don’t have it in me.”

    He slings his arm around Agatha. “Anyway, what do you think the reward is for today? Would kill for another chain restaurant, I’m not gonna lie.” It’s not another chain restaurant.

    “I’m sure you all are missing home,” Jeff tees up and Agatha can’t believe she forgot about the incoming sob fest. Now that she’s not pulling the fake husband move, it’s not quite relevant to her game. “Well, we brought some home to you.”

    At Jeff’s announcement, the tears start flowing on the beach. At Agatha’s side, Nick tenses.

    “You didn’t put your mom on your family list, did you?” Agatha hisses. “God, no,” Nick says. “It’s gonna be my dad or my brother. But they don’t —my dad isn’t good with words, like I said. I’m afraid he’ll say something that means well, but isn’t exactly…”

    “Todd,” Jeff is shouting, “say hello to your wife, Sh—”

    “I’m sure it will be fine,” Agatha says to Nick, ignoring Todd and whoever his wife is. “He loves you.”

    “Yeah,” Nick says, clearly trying to convince himself, “yeah, you’re right. He’ll be fine. Hey, you didn’t… your mom isn’t on your list, right?” Agatha laughs loudly over the sound of Abilash reuniting with his sister. “Trust me, kid,” she says, “I’d have to be in a dark ass place to ever invite that bitch onto the show.”

    “Good,” Nick says, laughing, “so who is on your list?”

    “Agatha!” Jeff calls. “I know you’ve missed your… good friend Ralph!” Agatha can’t help but grinning as she jogs over to Ralph. It’s nice not to have to kiss him on the mouth this time, instead giving him a hug as he whispers, “how’s the wife?” in her ear.

    The last time Ralph was here, Agatha was in the throes of it, so desperate to talk to someone about Rio that she ended up spilling her guts to Ralph on the Solomon Islands beach. He still gives her shit for it. She smacks him on the arm. “Good to see my husband again,” she says with a grin.

    Jeff laughs. “Rio, come say hi to your brother Javed!”

    Agatha holds her breath. She likes Javed. He was her favorite from when they spent Christmas at the Vidals this year. But he cannot show that he knows Agatha. Not when they’ve made it this far.

    Luckily, when Rio goes up to hug him, she whispers something in his ear and he nods seriously, barely even looking at Agatha.

    “Nick!” Jeff calls next. “Give a warm Survivor welcome to your dad, Isaac.”

    Agatha turns and looks. She can’t help it. She wants to see who this man is, who didn’t abandon his son, even when it cost him his marriage. It’s almost shocking how much Nick’s dad looks like him. Taller and gruffer, but undoubtedly family, smiling his own gap toothed grin when his son embraces him.

    Agatha looks away. She doesn’t want to think about it. Doesn’t want to think about what things could have been like if her own father had ever been around, hadn’t disappeared the second Evanora got pregnant. She wonders if she could have Nick’s childish goodness if she had even one parent who looked at her the way Isaac is looking at Nick now. Probably not.

    The reward challenge is for everyone to guide their blindfolded loved ones through a maze, something that Ralph is simply awful at. Agatha laughs a little at the sight of him, his overgrown hair sticking out from underneath his blindfold, stumbling around the maze.

    Needless to say, they don’t win. Wanda and her dumb brother pull it out, and she chooses John and Todd to go with her.

    “Everyone else,” Jeff says, “say one last goodbye to your loved ones and then head back to camp.”

    “Sorry I failed my wife,” Ralph says with an exaggerated frown. “But I think you’ve got better than me waiting at camp.”

    Agatha laughs and gives him a hug. She turns to go back to the rest of the tribe, when she’s stopped with a hand on her forearm.

    “Agatha, is it?”

    It’s Nick’s dad, Isaac, looking supremely uncomfortable.

    “That’s me,” Agatha says, wary of strange men touching her at any time. But he has Nick’s soft brown eyes.

    “I just wanted to say, uh,” Isaac says in a low voice, “we didn’t get too long to talk, but Nick… Nick says you’ve been really great to him. His mother wasn’t—well, it hasn’t been—these last few years… I just wanted to thank you.” Isaac moves his hand to Agatha’s shoulder. “For being kind to my son out here. He needs it.”

    Then, with a Nick-like smile, he’s gone, back with the rest of the family members.

    Agatha is struck with the overwhelming urge to weep, to curl up into a ball like when she was young and sob.

    But she can’t. She’s on national television.

    And the whole world is going to watch when Agatha won’t be able to save this man’s son.

    Day 30 “Agatha wins immunity!”

    It’s the first and only time Agatha isn’t overjoyed to hear those words. She drops the ball that she successfully balanced on a round piece of wood longer than anyone else and plasters a grin on her face. Jeff goes to place the necklace on her neck and Agatha’s heart sinks.

    Nicky, the clumsy idiot, dropped his ball five minutes into this challenge, and now here he is, on the cusp of elimination, while Agatha has to wear this tacky fucking necklace.

    “It’s okay,” Nicky says softly, as they walk back to camp, “I’m happy you have immunity.”

    “It’s not okay,” Agatha snaps. “We’re keeping you in this game.” Nicky swallows. “I really—I really appreciate all you and Rio have done for me, seriously I—”

    “Stop that,” Agatha says, “don’t get all sentimental on me. We aren’t at tribal yet.”

    ”We’ll still hang out after Survivor, right?”

    “Don’t talk like that,” Agatha says, voice desperate now. “Rio and I are doing what we can so you don’t get voted out. She’s working her magic to keep you in longer.”

    “My Survivor mommy and daddy,” Nicky says, laughing. “Always protecting me. But seriously, no matter what happens tonight, we’ll hang out after, right? Stay in touch?”

    “Of course,” Agatha says, trying not to well up. “They couldn’t pry me away from you.”

    As soon as they get back to camp, Agatha tries to get Rio alone, but Rio seems to be off with someone else at every second, going to the well with Todd and John, walking down the beach with Abilash, huddling by the shelter with Wanda.

    Finally, when the sun is starting to sink down, Agatha gets her alone down by the water, checking the nets for fish. As soon as Rio turns to fully face Agatha, Agatha’s stomach sinks.

    “I’ve tried,” Rio says, voice cracking. “I’ve been trying all afternoon to get the guys to turn on Wanda or anyone, but they won’t do it. Then I angled to have Wanda and John and Todd to vote for Abilash, but there's nothing. They say Nick has the jury and he’s a challenge threat and he’s aligned with you and that’s too dangerous. I’m—” Rio’s voice cracks. “I’m so sorry, sweetheart, I’ve tried.”

    Agatha, before she can stop herself, takes Rio in her arms, cradles her head as Rio breathes into Agatha’s neck.

    “I know,” Agatha says softly. “I know, my love.”

    They just stand there for a minute, water lapping at their feet, breathing into each other. When they finally part, Rio wipes her eyes.

    “He’s a really good kid,” she says. “I’m glad we added him into the fold.” “Yeah,” Agatha says, voice cracking, “me too.”

    Tribal council is dismal that night. The necklace is heavy around Agatha’s neck. For one split, stupid second she thinks about putting it on Nick’s neck, but to what end? To get herself voted out, leaving Nick to the vultures, just to be picked off next week? No. Agatha may be more sentimental in this moment than she cares to be, but she’s not stupid. Maybe, Agatha thinks, as Wanda drones on and on about how it’s so hard to vote off people you really like out here, there is some hope. They are at top seven. Agatha and Nick and Rio are all voting Abilash, so they only need one more person to flip. Maybe someone Rio talked to today, just one person, will make the difference and Nick will still be here. Just one sliver of doubt could keep Nicky here for just a few more days.

    “It is time to vote,” Jeff announces. “Rio, you’re up.”

    Agatha feels her leg shaking, sweat gathering on the back of her neck, unable to keep still. Even when she goes up to vote, her hand is unsteady as she writes down Abilash’s name.

    One vote, they just need one more vote. Stranger things have happened. “I’ll read the votes,” Jeff says.

    Agatha’s hand twitches on her leg, and Nick’s hand reaches out to grab it, holding her steady.

    “I’m okay,” he whispers, even as Jeff reads the first vote. “Nick,” Jeff says. “Abilash. One vote Nick, one vote Abilash. Nick.” Agatha swallows. People will be watching this. Maybe Nick’s disgusting mother will watch this, and feel some sense of glee, some satisfaction when he’s voted off.

    “Abilash,” Jeff says. “We are tied. Two votes Abilash, two votes Nick.” Maybe Agatha’s own disgusting mother will watch this too, and delight in Agatha failing once again.

    Agatha breathes in, breathes out. One more round of this. One more vote for each of them and then the deciding vote will be read. “Nick,” Jeff says. “Three votes, nick two votes Abilash.” Then Jeff picks up the next piece of paper, and takes a second longer. It’s the moment he takes before he announces who is eliminated, Agatha knows that, she’s seen it dozens of times. But the math doesn't add up. There should be one more vote in there for Abiliash. They have three. But then, too early, far too early, Jeff says, “the 12th person voted out of Survivor: Old Blood vs New Blood”—this can’t be right, where is the third vote for Abilash? Where is Rio’s vote?—“and the 4th member of our jury is Nick.”

    It doesn’t feel real. Even as Nicky gets up, still smiling, and pulls her into a hug, it doesn’t feel real.

    “Thank you, Agatha,” he whispers in her ear, “for everything.” She barely hears him. Her ears are ringing. Where was Rio’s vote? “Nick,” Jeff is saying, “the tribe has spoken.”

    Then poor Nicky’s torch is snuffed and he’s down the path and he’s eliminated from Survivor and he’s lost and Rio voted for him. The truth stirs like bile in her throat, desperate to come out. There were only two votes in that jar that weren’t for Nick. So Rio must have. Rio, who said she tried her best, who held Agatha tight on the beach this afternoon, voted for Nicky to go home. She sent him home, after everything. Agatha can barely look at her as they head back to camp. No one says much, which is good. Agatha is afraid that if she speaks, the only thing that will come out of her mouth is fire, the flame of Nicky’s torch that was snuffed out tonight. Snuffed by Rio.

    When they get back to camp, Agatha just sits by the fire while everyone else goes back into the shelter, the five other members of their tribe actually having space on the bamboo without Nick’s warm body there beside them. After a few long long minutes have passed, maybe an hour, Agatha watches Rio rise from the shelter, quietly walking over to Agatha at the fire. She looks sad, like she didn’t cast the killing blow herself. Agatha stands up, turns around, and makes her way into the jungle. She knows Rio is on her heels without even looking behind her. She waits until they are far enough into the woods for no one to hear them before turning around, staring into Rio’s big sad beautiful brown eyes and hissing, “what the fuck, Rio?”

    Rio’s hands and eyebrows both shoot up.

    “What do you mean?” she has the audacity to ask.

    “What do I mean?” Agatha scoffs. “You fucking voted for him, that’s what I mean!”

    “I’m sorry,” Rio says, defensively, “there was nothing I could do.” Agatha laughs. Oh, so there was nothing she could do? When she wrote down his name?

    “He got five votes, Rio.” Agatha hisses. “One of them was yours.” “He would have gone home either way,” Rio says, like she’s at a high school debate. Point-counterpoint, no weight behind it. “And the other group needs to think I’m voting with them, that I’ve turned on you. That’s the only way we get to the end.”

    “You said that you were going to protect him and you sent him home,” Agatha snaps. She thinks she’s crying. “I told him—I told him we would both save him and we—you voted him out.”

    “I wasn’t the deciding vote here, Agatha. I did everything to keep him here. Who was the one who gave him that idol at top nine? Me. And who took him on reward at top eight so he could be strong for the immunity challenge? Also me.”

    “Well it wasn’t enough,” Agatha snaps. Her voice sounds weak and needy to her own ears “He’s still gone. You still voted for him.” “I’m not the one wearing the necklace,” Rio says, voice growing harder. “If you really wanted to protect him, you could have given him immunity. But you’re still in the game and he’s not. That’s just how this game works. Sometimes it’s just people’s time to go.”

    “That’s fucking bullshit,” Agatha essentially screams. “We are in control of this game. We had a plan.”

    “And we tried,” Rio yells back. “I told you from the beginning that he didn’t fit in our plan.”

    “Oh, so now it’s his fault?”

    “No! Christ, Agatha, it’s no one’s fault. It’s just something sad that happened. It’s not your fault or his fault or my fault.”

    “Oh, but it is your fault,” Agatha says, cold and harsh, “you wrote his name on a little piece of paper and now the sweetest and brightest kid in the world has lost. What if someone had flipped? What if you wrote down his name and someone flipped?”

    “But they didn’t,” Rio says. “I knew they wouldn’t. Trust me, if there was any chance of him staying, I—

    “Trust you?” Agatha scoffs. “You want me to trust you? After tonight?” “Agatha, I didn’t lie to you! If I hadn’t voted for Nicky he would still be gone and I would next. Or you would be next. I did what I had to do. It didn’t change anything.”

    Oh, but it did. It changed everything. Agatha can barely stand to look at Rio. Maybe it’s irrational, maybe it’s not justified, but Nicky is gone, Agatha couldn’t save him, and Rio was on the wrong side of the vote. Rio takes in a deep, shaky breath. When she speaks, her voice is small. “Please. Please, sweetheart, I didn’t—”

    “Don’t,” Agatha snaps, “don’t call me that. I don’t—I promised him, Rio. I promised that we wouldn’t write his name down.”

    “I didn’t know that! You never told me. And you didn’t tell me to vote for Abiliash tonight.”

    “I didn’t think I would have to,” Agatha says. “I thought I fucking knew you. Guess I was wrong.”

    It’s a cheap shot, Agatha knows, but it feels good, in some twisted way, to see Rio’s face fall, to see tears come to her big brown eyes, so much like Nicky’s. To make someone else hurt the way Agatha has been hurting since Rio’s vote was read.

    “Agatha,” Rio is saying, “please just—”

    Agatha just shakes her head. She can’t do this anymore. The longer Agatha talks with Rio, the higher the chance that she will fold, that she will break down and fall into Rio’s arms and forgive her because she’s Rio, because she’s still here, because she’s always been here.

    But Agatha won’t do that. Nicky is gone and Rio wrote down his name and any hope of alliance and companionship on this fucking TV show is gone. Without saying another word, Agatha turns on her heel and walks away.

    Day 31 The rocks are hard and cold on Agatha’s feet, but she can’t find it in her to care. She carefully walks onto the rocks around Nicky’s cove and sits down, watching the sky lighten with the dawn.

    She can’t bear to be back at camp when everyone else wakes up, putting on the mask of indifference, having to avoid Rio’s eyes. Having to play this godforsaken game.

    So she sits and watches the dawn gently break, thinking about when she watched the sun set with Nicky all those days ago, when he opened up to her just for her to let him down.

    It takes until the sky has turned a gentle shade of pink for there to be footsteps next to Agatha on the rocks.

    Agatha squeezes her eyes shut. She can’t face Rio now, not on no sleep and no food, just this throbbing hurt in her chest.

    “Go away,” she snaps, not looking up.

    “Oh!” A voice that is distinctly not Rio’s says. “I… I’m sorry, I can leave.” Agatha looks up to see Wanda of all people, hovering a few feet from Agatha on the rocks.

    “I just wanted to say,” she says, “I didn’t know how much Nick—” “I don’t want to hear it,” Agatha says , looking back to the sea. She isn’t sure what’s worse, getting sympathy from this woman or the fact that people in this game know that she actually cares about something. “Okay,” Wanda says. “You know, when my husband—”

    “Nope,” Agatha says plainly.

    “Right. My sons actually—”

    “Nah,” Agatha says, wondering if she needs to push this bitch into the ocean to make her leave.

    “Got it,” Wanda says, still hovering. Then her tone shifts, all sympathy gone, just straight to the point when she says, “I want to vote out Todd next.”

    It works. Agatha snaps her head up.

    “Todd?”

    “Think about it,” Wanda says, sitting uninvited next to Agatha, “he has this whole moral superiority thing going on, and a bitter jury would vote for him over anyone who actually made a move.”

    Agatha thinks Todd is about as big of a threat as a senior dog with three legs, but the game talk hits like a cold shower after a hot day. “Why now?” Agatha asks bluntly. But she knows the answer. They needed to get rid of Nicky first. If Wanda thinks Todd is a jury threat, that’s nothing compared to Nicky’s integrity and charm and goodness. Agatha shakes it. Not now. She’s talking Survivor. “Top six is a hard time to make a move. Even number.”

    “Oh come on, Agatha,” Wanda says with a savviness that catches Agatha off guard. “Every tribal is the best time to make a move, you of all people know that. I wasn’t lying when I said my son was a big fan. I think he would kill me through the screen if I didn’t even try to work with Agatha Harkness on a blindside.”

    “Your gay son has excellent taste,” Agatha says, tossing her hair. It’s so blissfully easy to fall back into this, to fall back into the role of the villain who blindsides her opponents with a smile on her face and doesn't regret it. Who doesn’t grow attached to any other contestants, especially not in a way that would compromise her own game. “So how are you getting four votes against Todd?”

    “I don’t need four, really,” Wanda says. “Just three, and then we can go to rocks. John and Abilash would fold before risking their skin.” “So you plan on your three votes being…?”

    Wanda smiles at her. There’s something in that smile, something that Agatha recognizes. Rio wasn’t kidding when she said Wanda was a threat. Not that Agatha particularly wants to think about—

    “Rio,” Wanda says plainly. “Plus you. Your little fight might have fooled the guys, but…”

    Agatha’s jaw clenches. Not because Wanda saw through their move like that, but because she doesn't even know the half of it. That Rio’s act extended to who she voted for last night, that their “little fight” is far from little and far from staged. When Agatha closes her eyes, she can see Rio's unshed tears glistening in the moonlight last night.

    That’s why Agatha needs to play this game with eyes wide open right now. “Wow,” she drawls, blinking last night away away away, “you do have a brain under all that hair.”

    Wanda chuckles a little, but puts her hand up to her hair involuntarily. It’s the little things.

    “Would you be in?” Wanda asks. “Voting Todd out?”

    “Sure,” Agatha says airily. “Better him than me. But you’re gonna need something bigger than blind faith to make John and Ablilash turn on him. You’re going to have to humiliate him.”

    “How so?”

    Agatha grins. “Oh, your gay son is going to love this.”

    Day 32 “Can we talk?”

    “I’m a little busy here,” Agatha says, choosing to ignore Rio’s big sad eyes in favor of using the machete to chip away at the piece of wood that will soon become Todd Davis’ downfall.

    “Agatha, I just want to—can we clear the air?”

    “Air’s pretty clear,” Agatha says, still not looking at her. If she looks at her, it’s over. And Agatha needs to focus.

    Rio lets out a long suffering sigh.

    “At least let me help, okay?”

    Rio reaches out a hand, and Agatha flinches back, gripping the knife like a safety net. It does, tragically, make her look Rio in the face, which is, as predicted, a terrible mistake.

    Rio looks so sad and confused, like she is innocent in all of this, like she’s a harmless little lamb, not a stone cold killer.

    “I’m good,” Agatha snaps. “I don’t need your hands on this.” “Okay,” Rio says, holding her hands up. “Whatever you need.” She’s so damn understanding, it makes Agatha sick.

    “I’m actually making this idol for my new best friend, Wanda, so…” Rio rolls her eyes. Good. Agatha needs her mad.

    “Sure, Agatha, your new best friend. Who you’ll just blindside next tribal.” “Maybe,” Agatha says, shrugging a shoulder. “Or maybe I’ll keep working with her. She’s really smart, you know. You were right about that. And she has amazing hair. There’s so much of it.”

    Rio’s eyes are now narrowed, jaw tight. Perfect.

    “I know what you’re doing,” she says, voice still frustratingly even. “See if I care.”
